# Tankbook – Task Backlog

*`PHASES.md` broken into agent-sized tasks. Each task names its deliverable and the **checks that make it done** – a task without its checks green is not done, and checks land in the same PR as the code (TESTING.md levels: L1 unit · L2 contract · L3 sync-scenario · L4 UI/snapshot · L5 accuracy · M manual). Task IDs are stable – reference them in branches/PRs (`p0-4-grdb-migrations`).*

## Implied in every task's checks: it builds and it lints

The per-task checks below are **in addition to** the baseline gate, which is never restated per row because it applies to all of them: every tier a task touches must compile and its linter must exit **0** (`CLAUDE.md` hard rule 14, `docs/TESTING.md` → the baseline gate). Run `swiftlint lint` **from the repo root**, and judge it by exit code. A task whose feature tests pass but which leaves the build broken or the linter red is **not** done and does not get an `[x]`.

## Status legend

`[x]` done and independently verified – its own checks **and** the baseline gate re-run outside the implementing agent · `[~]` partially landed, gap named · `[ ]` not started · `[!]` **blocked on an action only the product owner can take** (a purchase, a legal decision, an account) - not work an agent can pick up · `[cut]` **decided against, with the reason recorded in the row and in the owning doc** – a cut task is not work waiting to be picked up, and re-proposing it needs the recorded reason to have changed. Status is recorded here so a fresh session knows what to pick up without re-deriving it.

## Version scope

*`CLAUDE.md` → Version scope is the convention. **A row without a marker is v1** – the launch build owes it, and the launch triage below says in which tier. Rows marked **[v1.0.x]**, **[v1.1]**, **[v1.x]** or **[v2]** are deferred to that release; the marker repeats the triage's target so a row can be read on its own. Section AG is v2 in its entirety.*

## Launch triage (2026-08-29) – what v1 needs, what waits

*Every open row (P-, W-, PR-, PJ-) sorted against one question: **can the app be submitted and
used by a stranger without this?** Three tiers. **Blocker** – submission is impossible or the
first session breaks a hard rule visibly. **Required** – a v1 promise (`VISION.md` MVP row, a
journey "always/never", or a written security/privacy commitment) that the code does not keep;
ship without it only with a written product-owner exception. **Deferred** – real, tracked, and
not a v1 condition; target named. Sizes are relative (S under a day of agent work, M a few days,
L a week-plus). Ordering inside a tier is the recommended sequence.*

**Two decisions this triage assumes, each reversible by the product owner:**

1. **The backend ships with v1.** `api.tankbook.live` is deliberately 502 today. Without it the
   app loses not only sync and cloud extract but **import parsing** (server-side by rule 9),
   **fresh exchange rates** (the seed pack ages from day one), remote config and the `appUpdate`
   notice – three MVP rows and the only way to retire a bad build. The backend is built and
   L2/L3-tested (P4, P5 complete); what is missing is the deployment itself, which no row tracks.
2. **v1 ships what is built, not only VISION's MVP column.** Service, reminders and sync are
   "v1.x" in `VISION.md` but P3 and P4 are complete; they ship, so their unreachable or
   half-wired parts count as required. EV charging (J6) and the Pro tier stay out.

### The v1.1 priority queue (product owner, 2026-08-31)

Eleven rows pulled out of the v1.1 bucket and **ordered**. Version markers are unchanged - these
stay `[v1.1]`/`[v1.x]` - this is a sequencing decision, not a scope change. Rows are marked
**PRIORITY** in place.

**Why these eleven.** Seven are the J7 cluster: P3 built the nouns (service entries, parts, tire
sets, reminders) and skipped most of the verbs that connect them, which is how the phase reads
COMPLETE while the journey does not hold together. Four are already-built machinery that nothing
calls - the cheapest rows in the backlog.

| # | Task | What the user hits today |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **PJ.28** | **Expense capture photographs the receipt and discards it.** The shutter behaves normally; `ExpenseEntryView` saves `attachments: []`. Nothing is shown, nothing is kept - the only row here that silently loses data |
| 2 | **PJ.22** | **The service log never proposes the next service.** No lifetime editor, so `proposedReminderId` is always nil. J7's promise is "the app proposes the next reminder"; today you log the past and remember the future yourself |
| 3 | **PJ.24** | **One door at completion.** The ReminderComplete sheet offers Type and Skip while the invoice is in the user's hand - hard rule 15 says the camera is a peer at every entry point |
| 4 | **PJ.23** | **A service line is frozen once saved.** Title, category and cost are uneditable, so a typo means delete and re-enter, losing attachments and links. `.other` "promoted later without data loss" has no promotion path |
| 5 | **PJ.26** | **Tires and their price never meet.** `purchaseExpenseId` exists in the schema and migration and is written **nowhere** - a tire set cannot say what it cost |
| 6 | **PJ.27** | **Nothing reminds you to swap back in spring.** A mount is recorded, then silence for six months |
| 7 | **PJ.25** | **The parts shelf is unreachable from the Garage** - it works, but only opens from inside a service entry |
| 8 | **PJ.19** | Station suggestion: the logic is **written** in `JOURNEYS.md`/`SCHEMA.md` and never called |
| 9 | **PJ.34** | F9a already ranks suggestions; the UI does not consume them, so receipt/QR date priority is computed and discarded |
| 10 | **PJ.35** | `.blobPrefetch` is a `PowerWorkKind` case **nothing produces** (P6.20) - photos never prefetch |
| 11 | **PJ.45** | `paceLimitKmPerDay` is a real per-vehicle field **edited nowhere in the app** |

Rows 8-11 are the pattern worth watching: **the mechanism exists, tested, and no user can reach
it.** Ask what produces a case, not only what handles it - the same question that produced P6.20.

I will do it anyway."*** The walk still HAPPENS - it is simply not a backlog item and does not gate the agent workflow, because it is the product owner's own work on their own device and nothing here can perform or verify it. **Kept, not deleted, because the journey list below is the checklist** and losing it would lose what the walk is for. **Nothing else in this file should treat SH.3 as a blocker.** If the walk turns something up, that files its own row with its own evidence - which is how RV.61, RV.65, RV.67 and RV.69 arrived.) | **Launch-readiness walk** on the TestFlight build, by a human, on the floor device: J1 (fresh install to first logged entry under 3 min, typed and scanned), J3 five live fill-ups (P2's M-check), J2 with the real MFM export, J10 one foreign receipt, J11a sign-in on a second device, F3 airplane mode through a full capture, F8 camera denied. Every screenshot EN and RU | Each journey's success metric recorded in `docs/STORE.md` with the build number; any failure files a task before submission |
| **[x]** SH.4 | **DONE 2026-09-01, verified.** Google is **wired, without the SDK** (product owner: a third-party SDK and its transitive dependencies are not worth one button). OAuth 2.0 authorization-code + PKCE through `ASWebAuthenticationSession`: `GoogleOAuth` (core, pure, 25 L1 tests) builds the request, validates the callback and shapes the exchange; `GoogleWebAuthenticator` (app) presents the browser and runs the POST on a bare `URLSession` - NOT `TankbookHTTPClient`, which rightly refuses `oauth2.googleapis.com` and must carry no Tankbook bearer. **The dead control is gone by construction**: the button renders exactly when `GoogleOAuth.Configuration.provisioned` returns non-nil, so a build with no client id offers Apple alone - measured, a Release build ships `GoogleClientID` empty. The J11a wrong-provider question is gated the same way. Mutation-checked: the `code_challenge` reverted to the verifier, and the `state` check relaxed to presence-only, each fail their test. **It also found and fixed a live account-takeover vector** - see the new `PR.35` row. Commit at merge | L4 `SignInUITests` 7/7 on `iPhone 17`; unit 1152; screenshots `SH.4-sign-in-google-{en,ru}.png` opened in EN and RU |
| **[x]** PR.35 | **DONE 2026-09-01, verified. Found while wiring SH.4.** `AppleGoogleIdTokenVerifier` verified an identity token's **signature** and never its `aud` or `iss` - and Apple's and Google's JWKS sign tokens for *every* client on their platforms. So any developer shipping an app with Apple or Google sign-in could collect their own users' id tokens and replay them at `POST /auth/session` to take over the matching Tankbook account. Live for Apple today, independent of Google. Now checked per provider against `Auth:AppleAudiences` / `Auth:GoogleAudiences` before any other claim is read, and it **fails closed**: an unconfigured allowlist refuses every token rather than accepting any. `Auth:Audience` is a different thing (the audience this server *stamps*) and `docs/STORE.md` §4.3 said to set it for this purpose - that sentence is corrected. **The class had no tests at all**: every L2 endpoint test injects `TestIdTokenSigner.Verifier`, a reimplementation that checks the signature and the expiry and nothing else. **Blocks `SH.1`** - the audiences must be set at deploy or every sign-in refuses | 14 new L2 tests against the REAL verifier; mutation-checked twice (fail-open on an empty allowlist, and dropping the per-provider split) each failing exactly its own test; backend 295, `dotnet format` 0 |

L1 tamper: edited document or signature rejected **on read** → bundled defaults; version below the Keychain floor rejected; expired document rejected; the server's transient empty-signature baseline (migration 003) rejected. L1: unknown key ignored for resolution, retained in cache, rest of the document applies. L1: a document setting one key leaves every other resolved value at its bundled value. L1: non-allowlisted host rejected by the HTTP client **and no `Authorization` header ever constructed for it**. L1: allowlist near-miss (`evil-tankbook.live`, `tankbook.live.evil.com`) rejected against a `tankbook.live` suffix rule. L1: failing candidate URL discarded, previous value stands |

| **[x]** P0.10 | **Payload contract** (`SYNC.md` → Payload contract and versioning): generate `docs/schemas/v1/*.schema.json` from the domain model; `payload_schemas` + `payload_migrations` tables seeded by migration; server-side envelope + JSON Schema validation on push with the typed rejection codes; iOS encode/decode against the same schemas; upcaster scaffold + fixture corpus | L1: every entityType has a v1 schema (build fails otherwise); every fixture validates against its version. L2: push rejects oversize/malformed/schema-violating payloads with the right code and JSON pointer; unknown entityType accepted unvalidated; `minSupported` gate returns 426 on push while pull still succeeds. L1+L2 parity: Swift upcaster and server declarative transform produce byte-identical output for every fixture. Round-trip: unknown fields and unknown entity types survive decode→encode unchanged |

## P1 · Local core loop

No step may read a decimal count or a position. Regression set: 033, 035, 037, 007 (the documented swap), the two pump/receipt pairs (029/040, 030/041 - each pair must resolve identically from both devices), the five Circle K screenshots | L1: 033, 035 and 037 resolve volume/price for the reason stated (marker vs band), pinned per fixture; **the mutations that matter**: rewriting every operand's decimals (3 -> 2, 2 -> 0) and swapping the operand order leaves every answer unchanged - a ladder that reads either fails these; a pair with no marker, no band and no history still returns nil; `receipt-007` does not regress. | **[x]** P2.14 | **`FuelExtractor.modal` is non-deterministic, and it destabilises every accuracy number in the project.** The total-finder resolves ties with `Dictionary(grouping:)`, whose iteration order Swift does not guarantee, and then `modes.max(by:)` on equal `primaryCounts` returns whichever element that order left last - with `?? modes.first` order-dependent too. So when two total candidates tie on count **and** on primary-label count, the answer is whatever the hash seed decided. Found by P4.13, which required three sweeps per image and watched the receipt score flip between **29/96 and 30/96** across identical runs (`receipt-021`, `-026`, `-029`). **This is not only a parser bug: `high-water.json` and `PumpPhotoGate.measuredHits` are gates asserted against a live score, so a tie-break that moves makes CI itself intermittently red - and it means the receipts mark cannot safely be raised from its stale 45/93 to the live 46/96 until this lands.** The fix must be deterministic **and the tie-break must be chosen deliberately and stated**, not just stabilised: the strongest candidate is to **return `nil`**, because the parser already refuses rather than guesses elsewhere (a printed `0.00` becomes `nil`, an unmarked operand pair returns `nil`) and hard rule 13 says a confident wrong value is worse than an empty field the user fills. If refusing lowers the score, that is **information, not failure** | L1: a fixture whose totals tie on both counts returns the **same** answer across 100 runs **in separate processes** - one process shares a hash seed and would pass while the bug is live; the chosen tie-break is asserted **by name** (nil, or the stated rule), not merely 'stable'; `receipt-021`/`-026`/`-029` are asserted individually. L5: re-score after the fix and **raise the receipts high-water mark in the same change**, with the new number proven stable across repeated runs. **Vacuous traps**: asserting determinism by calling the function twice in one process (same seed, always agrees); asserting 'a value is returned' rather than which; fixing `modal` alone and leaving `?? modes.first` order-dependent |

| **[cut]** P2.8 | ~~Foundation Models normalization layer (iOS 26+ gated)~~ **Cut 2026-08-25, not deferred.** The on-device model reports itself `unavailable` on an unsupported device language - the shipped iOS 26.5 SDK carries `SystemLanguageModel.UnavailableReason.unsupportedLanguageOrLocale` next to `supportedLanguages` - and **Russian is not in the supported set** (nor Kazakh). The corpus is 32 RU/KZ receipts and receipts score 41.6%, so the layer would have been unavailable exactly where the rules parser is weakest and available only where it already does best. The task's own check (*ships only if it strictly improves on an A/B against the corpus*) is therefore unsatisfiable in principle, not merely unmeasured - a stronger reason to stop than P2.7's 0/30. **Not blocked on the EU**, which has had Apple Intelligence since iOS 18.4 (April 2025); the constraint is language, not jurisdiction. Reasoning and the re-open condition (Apple adding Russian to `supportedLanguages`) live in `docs/VISION.md` -> "Why tier 2 was cut". Normalization for Russian receipts is now tier 3's job (the cloud LLM gateway) | **No code shipped, by decision.** The measurement that would have gated it cannot be taken |

| **[x]** P3.7 | **The tab bar double-counted the home indicator.** `AppTabBar.bottomPadding` is `28`, taken from `HomeA.dc.html`'s `padding: 10px 12px 28px` - but the artboard is a browser page with **no safe area**, so that 28 px *is* the designer's home-indicator allowance. The app then draws the bar through `safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom)`, which adds the real inset (~34 pt on iPhone 17) underneath it, leaving **~62 pt of dead space below the labels against the artboard's 28**. Spend the artboard's allowance **through** the safe area instead: `max(8, 28 - safeAreaInsets.bottom)` - ~42 pt on an indicator device, exactly 28 on an SE, and the 8 pt floor stops the labels touching the indicator on tall devices (the opposite mistake). `contentHeight` is derived from this padding, so `TabRoots.toastBottomClearance` and `contentBottomClearance` (used by `HomeView` and `TrendsView` to clear the raised capture circle) move with it. **General lesson worth carrying: an artboard's bottom padding is never a safe-area inset - porting it verbatim double-counts every time.** | L4: the existing "last row clears the tab bar" assertion still holds; a **device without a home indicator renders the artboard's exact 28 pt** and one with an indicator renders the floor, asserted against injected insets rather than a runtime measurement; screenshots in **both themes** (the defect was reported on the light shell) and EN+RU |

One gate: `AppStore.resetForTestsOncePerLaunch()` performs the wipe at most once per process; all five call sites (Home, RecentlyDeleted, TankLevel, TireSet, Reminder seeds) use it; the three per-type flags are gone. (2) The capture script's `en` branch passed no language arguments, so a preceding `ru` launch's `-AppleLanguages "(ru)"` persisted and `en` captures rendered Russian (P1.4-home, P3.2-parts-shelf). The `en` branch now passes `-AppleLanguages "(en)" -AppleLocale en_US` explicitly. Re-captured; the two known-bad files verified by size and re-run. | Seeds intact (no seed weakened or deleted, idempotence preserved); `swift test` 0 at 529+; `xcodebuild test` 0 at 108; `swiftlint` 0; EN captures no longer follow RU into Russian |

| **[x]** P4.10 | **LLM gateway server: `POST /extract`.** The endpoint is specified in `API.md` and its quota table (`llm_usage`) was migrated in P0.9, but **nothing builds the server that calls the model** - P6.3 is the client and assumes this exists. After the P2.8 cut this is the **only** model-assisted extraction path in the product, and the only one available to Russian receipts. Server-side provider key (hard rule 11 - an IPA is a zip), per-user quota from `llm_usage` returning the documented `402`/`429`, the 4 MB envelope cap, images processed **transiently and never written to disk or blob storage**, and a response that is exactly `ExtractionMeta` fields with per-field confidence. The server returns **field values, never domain judgements** - it does not decide mixedness, duplicates, or whether a fill is plausible (hard rule 9). **Device-side budget, normative and owned by `API.md` -> "The device's side of `/extract`":** the image is **downscaled and JPEG-compressed on device before upload**, and the device gives the round trip **3 seconds per attempt** before it stops making the user wait. On expiry the user proceeds with the locally recognized data and a message that names that next step (hard rule 7); a late answer may **only fill fields that are still blank and untouched**, marked as a suggestion (hard rule 13), and **never** changes anything after save (F4). | L2: `402` with quota exhausted and `429` per-period, both with the client falling back rather than erroring; oversize body rejected at the envelope, not the model; a request whose image is never persisted - assert the temp path and the blob bucket are both untouched after a successful call; **hard rule 12: no log line carries the image, the extracted values, or the prompt**. L1 (client): the 3 s budget fires deterministically against an injected slow transport and the sheet stays usable throughout; a late response fills a blank field and **cannot** overwrite a touched one; nothing arrives after save. **L5: compression must not cost accuracy** - re-score the receipt corpus through the compression step with the existing `AccuracyRatchetTests` scorer; hits may not fall below the recorded high-water mark, which is what stops "compress harder" from silently becoming "read worse" |
| **[x]** P4.11 | **`Date` does not round-trip exactly through persistence.** `Records.swift` stores every date as `timeIntervalSince1970`; adding the 978307200 s epoch offset shifts the fractional bits out of the Double mantissa, so a record read straight back after a write differs from what was written by **one ulp (~1.2e-7 s)** on `createdAt` / `updatedAt`. Found by P3.1a's round-trip test - the first test in the repo to assert **whole-record** equality rather than field-by-field, which is why it went unnoticed through P0.4. Measured severity: the drift is **idempotent** (a second round trip is stable), so sync's `updatedAt` LWW ordering is not at risk today - but the invariant "a record read back equals the record written" is one that sync, backup round-trips and the S1-S8 scenarios all lean on, and it is currently false. Fix is to store `timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate` (an exact Double round trip), which is a **column format change and needs a migration** - hence a task, not a patch | L1: a record of every entity type read straight back after a write equals what was written, whole-record and with no normalization; the existing sub-millisecond tolerance in `ServiceEntryTests` is **deleted** rather than loosened; migration applies forward over a seeded database and the migrated values are equal to the originals to the second |
| **[x]** P4.12 | **Corpus A/B against the cloud vision model, before P4.10 is designed.** Run 2026-08-26, full sweep (61 images, 0 errors), scored LLM-vs-rules with the one shared `CorpusScorer` and tolerance. **Result per class (rules -> LLM): receipts 46/96 -> 84/96, pump 1/46 -> 31/46, fiscal 1/3 -> 2/3, screenshots 7/24 -> 22/24.** Median latency 6.5-8.3 s, all above the 3 s device budget. The two silent failure modes are committed and asserted as named cases: `receipt-035` is the volume/price swap (70.44 read as litres) and `pump-009` is the decimal-separator shift (40.00 read as 400.0) - **both pass the arithmetic cross-check**. The probe's shift on `pump-005` did not reproduce: that fixture read exactly, three runs in a row, and `pump-009` flipped between correct and shifted across runs - the model is stochastic. **Recommendation (docs/EXTRACTION.md): the gateway cross-checks and suggests, never trusts** - a confident swap is worse than the parser's nil (hard rule 13), and the cross-check is scale- and swap-blind | L5: every class scored LLM-vs-rules with the same scorer and tolerance (offline, against committed `vision-ab/*.json`, no model call in CI); latency per image; the swap and decimal-shift cases asserted explicitly as known failure modes; a written recommendation in `docs/EXTRACTION.md`. `high-water.json` unchanged |
| **[x]** P4.13 | **PaddleOCR as a third extraction arm: embed it in Docker, measure it against the other two.** P4.12 measured the cloud VLM (receipts 84/96, pump 31/46, screenshots 22/24) but found its failures **silent** (swaps and scale shifts that pass the arithmetic cross-check), its output **stochastic**, and **every class median latency above the normative 3 s device budget** (pump peaks at 40 s). PaddleOCR is worth measuring because it attacks three of those four at once: self-hosted, so **no per-call cost and no third-party image sharing**, and plausibly far faster. **Russian is supported** (`cyrillic_PP-OCRv5_mobile_rec`; PaddleOCR-VL covers 109 languages) - the gap that killed the Foundation Models tier. Runs as a **container** (plain `docker run`, never compose) with ASP.NET reaching it behind an injected seam, exactly like `IBlobStorage` / `IApnsClient` / `IRateFeed`. **Two arms, because together they answer what neither answers alone**: **(A)** PP-OCRv5 Cyrillic recognition -> the EXISTING `FuelExtractor`, isolating recognition - if this scores about the same as Vision+parser it **confirms** `docs/EXTRACTION.md`'s central claim that interpretation is the bottleneck; if it scores far better, **that claim is wrong and the doc must be corrected in the same change**; **(B)** PaddleOCR-VL -> fields directly, competing with the DeepSeek arm on accuracy, latency, cost and determinism. Gateway-only: hard rule 1 means this can never be the primary path - on-device Vision stays tier 0. **Measured 2026-08-26:** Arm A (server det + `cyrillic_PP-OCRv5_mobile_rec`) -> `FuelExtractor` scored receipts **29/96** (rules 46/96, DeepSeek 84/96), pump **2/46** (1/46, 31/46), fiscal **1/3** (1/3, 2/3), screenshots **7/24** (7/24, 22/24) - **not** far better, so the interpretation-not-recognition split is **not falsified**, but the real finding is that the parser is **coupled to Vision's output**: PaddleOCR's detector merges `1,869 EUR/L` into one line (Vision splits it), and the Cyrillic-only reader misreads `ИТОГ` as `НТОГ`. PaddleOCR's OCR is **byte-identical across 3 runs**, but the sweep exposed a **pre-existing parser** non-determinism (`FuelExtractor.modal` ties two total candidates and breaks the tie via `Dictionary(grouping:)` iteration order) that flips `receipt-021`/`-026`/`-029` between runs (receipt score 29-30/96). Median latency 4.5-8.4 s, max 21.0 s - **above the 3 s budget in every class** even self-hosted. **Arm B (PaddleOCR-VL-0.9B) is blocked**: `paddlex` calls `is_bfloat16_supported()` with no place argument (raises on paddlepaddle 3.2.2 aarch64); forcing float32 OOM-kills the 7.6 GB Docker VM (exit 137). **Conclusion (docs/EXTRACTION.md): the ops cost is not justified** - Arm A is worse than on-device Vision, self-hosting buys no latency back without a GPU, and Arm B cannot run on modest hardware; PaddleOCR does not earn the fallback slot | L5: both arms scored with the **existing `CorpusScorer` at the same tolerance** (P4.12) - a second, subtly different comparison invalidates everything; per-class hits/total beside the rules and DeepSeek arms; **latency median and max per class against the 3 s budget**; **determinism measured, not assumed** - every image run 3x with variance reported, because the DeepSeek arm flipped `pump-009` between correct and shifted on re-run; scoring runs **offline against committed result files** so CI never needs the container; `high-water.json` unchanged. **Vacuous traps**: reporting only aggregate percentages (two silent failures matter more than a higher average); running each image once and calling it deterministic; scoring arm A against a different tolerance than the rules arm it exists to be compared with |

Measurement (median 6.5-8.3 s, max 40 s) is therefore not a contradiction; it is the reason the rule is written that way. While iOS Low Power Mode is on, the app postpones **opportunistic** work - launch/foreground/timer sync cycles, attachment upload and prefetch, the rate pack refresh, the catalog pack fetch - and changes nothing else. **The load-bearing distinction is background vs user-initiated**: a save, and any sync/restore/export/retry the user actually tapped, must run exactly as before, because a silently cancelled action the user is watching has no next step (hard rule 7) and reads as a hang. Resumes on `NSProcessInfoPowerStateDidChange`, not at next launch. Surfaces **only** as a reason on S7's existing passive status row ("Waiting to sync - 5 changes - Low Power Mode is on") - reassurance, never a warning: no amber, no badge, no toast, no modal (hard rule 8). L1: resume fires on the power-state change, not only at launch; the deferred queue then drains. L1: a save, an edit and a delete are unaffected while the mode is on (hard rule 1 - no screen is ever sync-gated). L1: blob upload/prefetch defers **without** losing the pending record. L4: the status row shows the reason in EN+RU and is **not** amber; it vanishes when the mode ends. **Vacuous traps**: a gate consulted but not awaited; asserting only that the mode was read; testing the defer path without the run path (an implementation that always defers passes); a test that reads the real `ProcessInfo` and therefore only passes on the machine that ran it |

**`api.tankbook.live` does not answer yet, and that is intentional** (product owner, 2026-08-28). nginx proxies it and returns
**502** because no backend is listening on the upstream port. Postponed deliberately, not broken: the app is local-first, so an
unreachable API costs sync, cloud extract and import parse and **nothing else** - every screen keeps working offline (hard rule 1).
Two consequences worth knowing while it stays down: the config guardrail's `GET /health` probe cannot promote a candidate
`apiBaseUrl`, so the bundled default stands; and P6.18's update requirement resolves `.none`, because a config document that never
arrives carries no `appUpdate` - which is the fail-open rule behaving exactly as designed.

## PJ · Journeys review (2026-08-29)

*Source: `docs/JOURNEYS.md` reviewed stage by stage against the tree at `93d2619` by four
read-only passes (brief `agents/briefs/REVIEW-JOURNEYS.md`); every bug-level claim below was
re-verified by hand before it was written. Gaps already tracked elsewhere are cited, not re-filed:
PR.2 (sign-out revoke), PR.7 (auto-retry), PR.13 (offline vs server-down), PR.14 (S1 row + S7
toast - its deliverable should also name Recently deleted's "Overwritten by sync" section and
Compare, both fixtures), PR.22 (LLM quota note), PR.30 (reminder hours), P2.6/P2.7/P5.5b/P6.9
open halves. J6 (EV charge) and J12 (second driver) are v1.x/v2 per `VISION.md` and are not
filed, except for the one live defect J6 leaves in v1 (PJ.12).*

*Five ticked rows claim more than the code does; PJ.47 footnotes them: **P1.1** (Welcome root
and its three edges were never built), **P2.2/P2.3** (parser ported, sheet takes a prefill - the
seam camera -> OCR -> prefill -> sheet was never a task), **P3.4** (Reminders has no production
entry point), **P4.4** (wrong-provider detection exists, the entry that arms it does not),
**P5.2** (S8 backfill runs only under a DEBUG flag), **P6.3** (only the timeout branch of F4
renders).*

*The shape of the finding, in one sentence: the Confirm sheet, the engines and the import review
are built and L4-covered, but **the scan door is painted on** - no camera or Photos image ever
becomes a `ConfirmPrefill` outside launch-argument seeds - and four screens that the journeys
route through (Welcome, Reminders, the S5 notice, the post-sign-in push) exist only behind
`-force*` fixtures.*

| ID | Task | Checks (done when...) |
|---|---|---|

L4 `ExpenseCaptureUITests`: scanning a shop receipt lands a title the user can accept with one tap, and Save is reachable without typing. L4: with no merchant found, the form names the missing field. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the title field is non-empty without asserting it came from the RECEIPT; loosening the save gate and calling the seam closed; a fixture whose merchant line is also its category name |
| **[ ]** PJ.51 | **The listing promises what PJ.49 and P5.4b do not ship.** Registered 2026-09-06 as the consequence of scheduling EV logging at v2: both store descriptions say "Petrol, diesel, hybrid and electric in one history" and the EN keyword field carries `ev` (`docs/STORE-COPY.md`, `docs/STORE.md` §§2-4), while an EV cannot log a charge (PJ.49); and both descriptions name **six importers** where one parser exists (`backend/src/Tankbook.Api/Import/MfmParser.cs`; P5.4b is `[v1.1]` and blocked on real export fixtures). The repo has already corrected this exact class three times - the Welcome tagline (PJ.3b), the site copy (W6), the capture caption (PJ.12b) - and the rule each time was the same: **the copy names what the build does** **The fix:** **Before submission, one of two things**: ship the paths, or change the words. For EV: drop "electric" from both descriptions and `ev` from the EN keyword field, and say in the RU listing what is actually true. For importers: name the one that works rather than six, or say plainly which are coming. **This is a copy change, not a feature cut** - the roadmap page is where "coming" belongs (`site/content/roadmap.md`) | The submitted description names no capability the build lacks: grep the final copy against the shipped capture modes and the shipped parsers, and record the check in `docs/STORE.md`. **Vacuous trap**: fixing the English and leaving the Russian, which is where the same sentence also appears |
| **[ ]** PJ.52 | **[v2]** **The parts shelf J6 already describes, and the schema already carries.** Raised by the product owner 2026-09-06 as promising, and the groundwork is done: `ServiceRecord.usedParts: [UUID]` links `Expense(.parts)` entries installed in a service ("links, not costs", `docs/SCHEMA.md`), `ServiceItem.partNumber` exists explicitly to "enable reorder and lifetime tracking", and `.parts` is a category on both `ServiceCategory` and `ExpenseCategory`. What is missing is the SHELF: a part bought now and fitted later has nowhere to wait, so today it is either an expense with no destination or a service line typed twice. J6 already has the journey and its metric ("shelf-suggested parts accepted >=40%"), and its fallback is already correct - "the shelf is an optimization, never a gate". **What competitors do here, measured 2026-09-06**: aCar records part specifications and MyFuelLog2 records purchased parts with manufacturer and place bought, but **nobody in the category ships a real inventory** - no stock counts, no fitted-state, no lifetime. It is genuinely unowned, which is the same reason to be careful: it may be unowned because owners do not want it. **The fix:** **A part is bought, then fitted** - two events on one row, not two records. Buying is the expense that already exists; fitting attaches it to a `ServiceRecord` through `usedParts`, and the shelf is simply the parts bought and not yet fitted. **Do not build a warehouse**: no quantities, no reorder points, no suppliers - the moment it needs a stock count it has become a different product (`docs/VISION.md` scope). **Prove the demand before the depth**: the cheapest version is the service form suggesting parts already on the shelf, which is exactly what J6's metric measures, and if acceptance is under that bar the feature is answered rather than expanded. Lifetime ("this filter lasted 18 400 km") derives from the odometer span between fitting and replacement, the same way `TireSet` mileage already does - reuse that, do not invent a second derivation | L1: a `.parts` expense with no service is on the shelf; attaching it to a service removes it and leaves the cost on the expense, never duplicated into the service total (hard rule 4's sibling - one cost, one place). L1: fitted-to-replaced odometer span yields the part's lifetime, and an unknown span says "unknown" rather than estimating (the `TireSet` rule). L4 `ServiceEntryUITests`: the form suggests a shelf part and one tap fits it. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the shelf list renders rather than what fitting CHANGES; double-counting the cost and calling it linked; a fixture where the part was bought and fitted the same day, which hides the whole point |
| **[ ]** PJ.53 | **[v1.x]** **There is no widget, no Shortcut and no lock-screen door - the habit surface the whole capture design assumes.** `VISION.md` lists "Widgets, Shortcuts, Siri" at v1.x and J3's opening beat is literally "lock-screen widget / app opens on capture", yet `project.yml` has **no widget extension target** (verified 2026-09-06) and the app donates no App Intent. J3's metric is "median capture-to-save under 15 s", which is unreachable while reaching the camera means finding an app icon. Competitors ship this: widgets in aCar, Fuelio and My Cars; Siri Shortcuts in Road Trip MPG (`docs/COMPETITORS.md`). The core was built as a reusable package for exactly this (P0.1: "widgets/extensions can reuse it"). **The fix:** **An App Intent first, a widget second.** `LogFillUp` (and `TypeItManually`) as App Intents makes the action available to Siri, Spotlight, the Shortcuts app and the Action button at once, and a widget is then a launcher for the same intent - one action, several doors. **Both doors, as always** (hard rule 15): the widget carries capture AND type-it, not capture alone. **Decide what an intent may WRITE**: the honest answer is nothing - it opens the app on the right screen - because a fill-up saved without the user seeing the fields is hard rule 13 inverted. Say so in the row when you build it | L4: the widget's two controls open the capture surface and the manual form respectively. L1: the App Intent is donated and resolves without the app running. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the widget target builds; a widget that only launches the app with no destination; shipping capture-only, which makes the widget a rule-15 seam of its own. Screenshots of the widget in both sizes, EN+RU |
| **[ ]** PJ.54 | **[v1.x]** **Nothing offers to log the fill-up you just made.** The moment a fill-up is knowable is when the driver gets back in and drives off, and the app is silent then; the user has to remember, later, at a screen. This is also the only credible attack on the **odometer friction** `VISION.md:191` names - the one field no receipt carries - because the prompt can arrive while the car is still around the user. Depends on PJ.53's App Intent: without it there is nothing for an automation to run. **The honest scope, and its limit:** **the trigger is a CarPlay or car-Bluetooth DISCONNECT, and the user wires it.** iOS personal automations are the user's to create - an app cannot install one silently - so what we ship is the intent, an "Add to Shortcuts" affordance, and copy that explains the recipe in one line. **"Near a fuel station" is NOT in this row and should not be smuggled into it**: there is no Shortcuts trigger for "any fuel station", so it would need CoreLocation region monitoring - the app imports no CoreLocation today, and adding it changes the privacy nutrition label we just submitted (`docs/STORE.md` §6 declares no location collected). That is a product decision with a store consequence, not an implementation detail; if it is ever wanted it gets its own row and its own argument. **The fix:** ship the intent and the recipe; measure whether people wire it before considering location | L4: the intent runs from the Shortcuts app and lands on the capture surface with the car preselected. L1: the intent is available with the app backgrounded. Manual check on the floor device: create the disconnect automation, drive, and confirm the prompt arrives once and not on every stop. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the shortcut appears in the app's own UI rather than in Shortcuts; claiming a location trigger that CoreLocation absence makes impossible; a prompt that fires on every Bluetooth disconnect, which trains the user to dismiss it |

## RV · Reviewer bugs (first TestFlight build, 2026-09-02/03)

*Found by running build **1.0.0+466** on a real device against the live backend, and by reading
production logs. Every row here is something no test caught, because each sits in a seam the suites
do not span: a client/server contract, a UIKit lifetime, a tier default, or a screen nobody drove on
hardware. `RV.1`-`RV.5`, `RV.7`-`RV.9` and `RV.11`-`RV.16` are fixed; `RV.10`, `RV.17`-`RV.20`, `RV.22`-`RV.26` and `RV.28`-`RV.35` are open.*

| # | Bug | Check |
|---|---|---|
| **[x]** RV.1 | **The rate pack 400'd on every launch.** `RateStore` asked for a **two-year** window; the server caps at `Rates:MaxPackDays` **400**, so every refresh was rejected - five in one session, and it would have been every launch forever. A number in two places that disagreed (`PRACTICES.md`). Invisible because the client tests use a `RateFetcher` double that accepts any range and the server tests choose their own: **both sides tested, the contract between them not** | The client's requested span is recorded and compared against the server cap; mutation-checked - restoring the two-year window fails with `730 <= 400` |
| **[x]** RV.2 | **Choosing a photo from the library did nothing** - the app stayed on the capture screen. `PHPickerViewControllerDelegate` set `isPresented = false` first, which tears down the representable and releases the `Coordinator`, so the `[weak self]` in the async `loadObject` callback was already nil and the pick was dropped. A **race**, which is why review and the simulator missed it: a small local image can beat the teardown, an iCloud photo that must download never does | `self` is captured strongly (a `@MainActor` class is `Sendable`), so delivery no longer depends on how long the load takes. Needs a device re-test with an iCloud photo |
| **[x]** RV.3 | **The per-request log line buried everything else.** One `http.request` at Information per call, several per screen, hiding `auth.session`, `sync.push`, `blob.commit` | Success is `Debug`, 4xx `Information`, 5xx `Warning`, plus a readable summary. Re-levelled, not removed: a test pins that the successful line still appears at `Debug` |
| **[x]** RV.4 | **DONE 2026-09-03, product owner: the free tier gets cloud extract in v1.** The allowance was 0, so `POST /extract` answered 402 to every user - correct against `API.md`'s "LLM gateway (Pro)", except Pro is cut from v1 (`P6.16`), so the gate applied to nobody and refused everyone while the served config document promised `llmQuota.cloudFallback: 50`. Both halves were individually right and the pair was a lie. Free now gets exactly the advertised 50/day, from a named constant, and it stays configuration (`LlmGateway__TierRequestsPerPeriod__free`) so the number can move without a deploy. The alternative was capture on on-device Vision alone - **38.3%** on receipts against the cloud model's 84/96 (`P4.12`) | 3 L1 tests: free gets the advertised number, free is entitled **at all** (asserted separately - an equality check against a zero constant would still pass at zero), and an unknown tier is still refused. Mutation-checked: restoring 0 fails two of the three |
| **[x]** RV.5 | **DONE 2026-09-03. No preview or confirmation after a capture.** A shot is taken and the flow moves on with nothing shown; the user cannot see what was captured, accept it, or re-take. Requested by the product owner from the device walk. Touches `JOURNEYS.md` (J1/J3), `SCREENMAP.md` (a screen between capture and Confirm) and `ERRORS.md` (the re-take affordance), so the docs move with it - and hard rule 15 applies: re-take and "type it instead" are peers, not a failure branch | DONE: `CaptureReviewView` is a full-screen cover between both capture doors and the Confirm sheet - the image fitted (never cropped), **Use this** (which is where the recognition pipeline now runs, so a re-take costs no OCR), **Re-take** (back to the live camera, nothing kept) and **Type it** as its equal-width neighbour. `CaptureReviewUITests` (4 tests, run): the shot is shown and both verdicts are reachable, Type it is a peer by frame geometry and opens the manual form, Use this reaches Confirm with the pipeline's real litres, and Re-take returns to the camera and **does not** present Confirm. `JOURNEYS.md` (J1, J3, J3b), `SCREENMAP.md` (graph, inventory, dead-end audit, a section for the step) and `ERRORS.md` (Capture) moved with it; screenshots EN and RU |
| **[x]** RV.6 (2026-09-04, verified by orchestrator: swift build 0, `swift test` **1250/117**, localization gate 0, app build 0, zero lint errors outside the sibling worktree; mutation-checked personally - restoring the unconditional refetch fails 3 tests including the round-trip count assertion, restored byte-identical. Commit `48d9313`. **INVESTIGATED 2026-09-04, read-only pro agent. Verdict: PARTIALLY fixed - the automatic burst is gone, a navigation shape remains, and TWO claims in this row's own text are wrong.** **(1) RV.18 fixed the automatic re-poll**, which was the most probable source of the production log: pre-RV.18 every `scenePhase == .active` transition ran a full opportunistic cycle -> `runSync` -> `refresh()` -> a devices fetch, and RV.18 measured launch alone firing **twice ~0.6 s apart**. `runOpportunisticSync` now returns early inside 30 s, so that shape is impossible. **(2) A different shape still reproduces, and it is not a poll at all**: `AppSync.refresh()` re-fetches the device count **unconditionally on every call** (`AppSync.swift:312-315`; `fetchedDeviceCount` is set to nil and re-fetched, never reused), and `SettingsView`'s `.task` calls `refresh()` on **every appearance** - ungated. So one "check my devices" round trip costs **three** fetches: Settings appears (#1), push AccountDevices (#2, `didLoad`-guarded so once per push), pop back and Settings `.task` re-runs (#3). Gear -> account -> back -> account is four real GETs in well under 14 s with no sync cycle involved. **There is no de-duplication anywhere**: no in-flight guard, no minimum interval, and RV.18's 30 s gate covers only `runOpportunisticSync`, not this path. **CORRECTION 1 - this row's premise is factually wrong**: *"the per-IP limits key on it"* - there is **no rate limit on `GET /account/devices`**. `Program.cs:584` maps it with no `.RequireRateLimiting` while six neighbouring groups have one, and `API.md`'s table lists limits only for `auth/session`, `auth/refresh`, `import/parse`, `extract`, `sync/push`, `blobs/begin` and `feedback`. The real cost is battery, plus the future risk if a limit is ever attached to this un-throttled GET. **CORRECTION 2 - RV.22 did NOT worsen it**, which was the orchestrator's stated suspicion when briefing: `SyncStateChip` reads only the computed `surfaceState` plus stored counts, never calls `refresh()` or `devices()`, and is gated to the active tab root. **Also noted**: RV.26's refresher can double a devices GET on a 401 (the screen's client wires it, the count path deliberately does not) - real, but not the polling shape.**) | **`GET /v1/account/devices` is polled repeatedly** - four calls in 14 seconds (21:07:23, :28, :30, :37), then more. Looks like a screen refetching on every appearance rather than on change. Cheap per call, but it is the shape that becomes a battery and rate-limit problem, and the per-IP limits key on it **ACCEPTANCE CORRECTED 2026-09-04 by the investigation**: "fetch on appear and on an explicit refresh" is what `AccountDevicesView` **already does** (`didLoad`-guarded, one fetch per push) - so the original acceptance would have passed against the bug. The residual repetition is the **sync surface's count fetch piggybacking on every `refresh()`**, which the row never named. The fix is there: either reuse `fetchedDeviceCount` and re-fetch only when nil (clearing it explicitly on sign-in, sign-out and after a revoke/delete), or drop the count fetch from `refresh()` entirely and let the AccountDevices screen be the sole fetcher - collapsing the round trip from three fetches to one. **The test must assert a REQUEST COUNT over a recording transport** - `GET /v1/account/devices` fires <= 1 across `Settings -> AccountDevices -> pop -> AccountDevices` - never "the screen loaded", which passes against the bug. The seeded `AccountStubTransport`/`SeededLaunchTransport` already serve this path, so the assertion is cheap | The device list is fetched on appear and on an explicit refresh, not on every re-render; L4 asserts the call count across a navigation cycle |
| **[x]** RV.7 | **DONE 2026-09-03.** `RatesHostedService` now runs one pass BEFORE entering the timer loop. `PeriodicTimer.WaitForNextTickAsync` waits a full interval before its first tick, so with the 6-hour default there was no pass for six hours after a start - and every deploy restarts the process and resets that clock, so deploying more often than six-hourly meant it **never ran**. That is what production showed: `/v1/rates` served a correctly-shaped, permanently empty pack while answering 200, so nothing looked wrong. The pass is idempotent - the job's own comment calls the interval "a liveness knob, not a correctness one" - so one extra run at boot costs a query and removes the dependence on uptime | L2 drives `StartAsync` with a 24-hour interval, so no tick can arrive, and asserts the feed is reached. **The first version of this test was VACUOUS** - it called `RunOnePassAsync` directly, so deleting the startup call from `ExecuteAsync` still passed; it now drives the real entry point and the mutation fails it |
| **[x]** RV.8 | **DONE 2026-09-03. A ten-second wait with nothing on screen.** Production measured `llm.extract DurationMs=10218` on a real receipt; the Confirm sheet showed **nothing at all** for the first 3 s and a **motionless hourglass** for the remaining 7, which reads as a finished statement rather than as work in progress. The gateway session already knew - `phase` has carried `.running` since P6.3 - but only `.budgetExpired` was ever rendered, so the state that covers the whole wait was the one state with no view. Reported by the product owner from the device walk | `GatewayReadingBanner` renders `.running` and `.budgetExpired` as one banner in two states (same shape, same place, different words), both carrying the small `inkSoft` `ProgressView` the rest of the app uses, degrading to the static hourglass under Reduce Motion. L4 asserts the running banner is on screen BEFORE the 3 s budget and the timeout banner after; screenshots EN and RU |
| **[x]** RV.9 | **DONE 2026-09-03. An attachment could not be opened.** The receipt strip rendered a 44x56 thumbnail that was **decorative by construction** - `AttachmentPhotoChip`'s own doc comment said so - so no tap target in the app opened a receipt at full size and there was no zoom. The photo the entry exists to evidence could only be squinted at. Reported by the product owner: *"I want to look deeper"* | DONE: the chip is now a `Button` opening `AttachmentViewerView`, which handles four states - **local** (fitted, pinch/pan/double-tap zoom via a `UIScrollView`), **fetching**, **unavailable** (signed out, fetch failed, or unreadable bytes - each naming its next step, hard rule 7), and **PDF**, rendered with `PDFView` rather than refused, because a service invoice IS a PDF and "cannot be previewed" would be a dead end for the document the feature is for. `BlobService.localData(for:)` added as the synchronous network-free "is it here already" check; delivery stays `LazyBlobFetcher` (cache-first, verify-on-download). `AttachmentViewerUITests` 5 tests, plus `AttachmentSyncUITests` and `EditEntryUITests` re-run (17 total). Red-before-green verified: reverting the chip to the decorative version fails two of them, because `app.buttons[...]` does not match a non-control - which is precisely the bug. `ERRORS.md` (4 rows), `SCREENMAP.md`, `SYNC.md` (Delivery) and `JOURNEYS.md` (new J8b) moved with it; screenshots EN and RU for both the viewer and the not-downloaded state. **Zoom magnification is asserted only as "the photo survives a pinch"** - XCUITest cannot read `zoomScale` - and both not-downloaded screenshots show the fetch-failed variant, not the signed-out one |
| **[x]** RV.10 | **The date picker cannot be dismissed without changing the date.** **Screen named by the product owner (2026-09-03): edit / add an entry from the Log screen**, so this is `ManualFillUpDateRow` as used by `EditEntryView` and `EditEntryNonFillView` - not the four-screen guess this row started as. The row expands the graphical picker inline and the header button toggles it shut, so a dismiss path EXISTS; what is missing is a way to find it. The tap target is the date text at the far end of the header, the chevron never flips to point up, and once the calendar is open the eye is on the calendar - so "tap the date again" is discoverable only if you already know it. Treat this as an affordance bug, not a missing feature | L4 on Edit entry: opening the picker and dismissing it leaves the date **byte-identical**, and the dismiss affordance is reachable without touching the calendar. A test that changes the date first proves nothing - the bug is specifically the no-change exit  **DONE 2026-09-03.** Treated as the affordance bug it is, not a missing feature. The **whole header row** is now the toggle (`.contentShape(Rectangle())`, so the spacer between label and date answers a tap too - the eye lands on the row's left half once a calendar is up, and only the right half responded before), and the chevron **flips to `chevron.up`** while the picker is open, putting the one collapse cue directly above the calendar. Dismissing never touches the date binding, so a value the user did not choose is never written (hard rule 13). Verified by the orchestrator: swiftlint 0 errors, `swift build` 0, `EditEntryUITests` **12 tests 0 failures**; **both screenshots opened personally** - the flipped chevron is visible in EN and RU, and RU's longer "2 сент. 2026 г." still fits the row. A `-openDatePicker` DEBUG hook was added for the capture, since `simctl` cannot tap |
| **[x]** RV.11 | **DONE 2026-09-03. The "Attach receipt" chooser opened at the top of the screen while its button was at the bottom.** iPhone-only, which killed the first hypothesis: a top-anchored chooser is the iPad popover presentation, and this app is `TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY: "1"`. The screenshot settled it - **iOS 26 renders `confirmationDialog` as a popover anchored to the view the modifier is attached to**, and it was attached to the ScrollView, so the arrow pointed at the middle of the form. Under iOS 18 the same modifier is a bottom action sheet that ignores the anchor entirely, which is why this shipped: the anchor became load-bearing only on the newer runtime, and the suite asserted only that "Photos" existed - true throughout the bug | DONE: the modifier moved onto the row that opens it (ConfirmManual) and onto the card that carries the button (Edit entry). No screenshot: `simctl` cannot tap, and the measured geometry is stronger evidence than a still frame would be |
| **[x]** RV.12 | **Saving a captured entry drops the user back into the camera.** Capture a receipt, the Confirm sheet pre-fills, tap Save - and the capture window opens again, so a completed entry looks like a failed one and a second tap starts a second entry. **Diagnosed:** nothing navigates - capture is a MODAL over the current tab, and `ManualFillUpView.save()` dismissed the Confirm sheet only, uncovering the capture modal that was never torn down (`SCREENMAP.md` -> Saving inside capture). Reported by the product owner from the device walk. **Overlaps RV.5**: with a before-capture screen in place the destination after save is a decision that has to be made once, for both, so fix them together | L4: capture -> Confirm -> Save lands on the entry's home surface, and the camera is NOT presented; driving Save twice cannot create two entries  **DONE 2026-09-03** (committed `cb004c6`; the row was left unticked at the time). Capture is a MODAL over the current tab, so `save()`'s `dismiss()` closed the Confirm sheet and uncovered a camera that was never torn down. An `onSaved` closure supplied by the capture side now tears the cover down, so `ManualFillUpView` never learns about tabs; the `catch` branch is untouched, so a failed save leaves the photo and the typing exactly where they are. Both doors covered. Verified by the orchestrator: 41 UI tests across the four capture suites |
| **[x]** RV.13 | **DONE 2026-09-03. The outbound HTTP logs were the inbound noise all over again.** RV.3 re-levelled the inbound `http.request` line; the **outbound** `HttpClient` logs were untouched and still Information - `RequestPipelineStart`, `RequestStart`, `RequestEnd`, `RequestPipelineEnd`, **four lines per call**, so one `/extract` cost eight, and they rendered their own placeholders literally (`{Uri}`, `HttpMethod={"Method":"GET"}`), reading as a broken logger. **The unrendered URI was never the bug**: an outbound URI is a domain value, so hard rule 12 forbids it and the placeholder is accidentally correct - the fix removes the lines rather than filling them in. `System.Net.Http.HttpClient` is now `Warning` in both committed templates (the base one also covers the `Testing` environment, which has no settings file of its own). Checked before silencing: every outbound caller logs its own failure - rates a `Warning`, the LLM gateway `llm.extract` at `Error`, APNs onto `sync.nudge`'s counts, and a throwing JWKS fetch as `error.unhandled` at `Error` - so nothing depended on the HttpClient lines, which narrate a throw only at Information themselves | 2 L2 tests in `LevelDisciplineTests`, both driving a real `IHttpClientFactory` call over the **shipped `appsettings.template.json`** - the bug is in configuration, so a test that builds its own filters would pass forever. A 200 emits no `System.Net.Http.HttpClient` line while the caller's own `rates.fetch` is asserted PRESENT in the same window (otherwise the test passes against a mute pipeline); a refused connection through the real `CisRateFeed` still surfaces the caller's `Warning`. Mutation-checked: removing the config entry fails both |
| **[x]** RV.14 | **The device pushes its own records back in a loop.** Vehicle `01a063ea...` was pushed six times in seven minutes (22:05:39, 22:07:17, 22:08:35, 22:09:13, 22:11:10, 22:12:50), `Accepted` and `Conflicts=0` every time, and each `sync.pull` returns exactly what the previous push wrote - `Returned` and `NextSince` track the SCN the push just assigned. The device pulls back its own writes and re-dirties them, so the SCN grows forever on an idle account. Two paths in `SyncEngine.applyPull` would do it, both reachable for a `Vehicle`: `case .local` re-dirties whenever `local.record.payload != remote.payload`, a **raw payload comparison** that never converges under a lossy round-trip (key order, decimal formatting, server normalisation); and `case .fieldMerge` writes `syncState: .dirty` **unconditionally**, and Vehicle is the one type that computes `fieldVersions` first, against a `payloadMemory` that may not survive a relaunch. **Which of the two is unproven** - the log shows the loop, not its cause | L2: one record pushed, then pulled back unchanged, must settle to `.synced` and push no second time. Mutation-checked: re-introducing the unconditional `.dirty` fails it. The vehicle case must be driven with an EMPTY `payloadMemory`, which is the state after a relaunch  **CONFIRMED AGAIN 2026-09-03 07:03-09:49 with a second, worse capture**: vehicle `01a063ea...` pushed **12 times in under three hours**, `Accepted` and `Conflicts=0` every time. Two escalations the first capture did not show. (a) **The second vehicle joins the loop the moment it exists** - `01a066a9-a76a...` (the car added in RV.25) pushes at 09:47:44, 09:48:19 and 09:49:42, so this is every Vehicle, not one poisoned row. (b) **`preferences` loops too** - the singleton `00000000-0000-7000-8000-000000000001` pushes at 09:47:44 and again at 09:48:19 with a pull between them. Preferences is NOT a Vehicle, so it never reaches `mergeVehicle`; it goes through record-level LWW and the **`case .local` raw-payload comparison** this row lists as the other candidate. That arm was scoped OUT of RV.14 as "a real fragility but not what drives this loop" - **the log says it is live as well**, so the second half needs its own row or an explicit widening of this one.  **DONE 2026-09-03.** `mergeVehicle` now reports `.fieldMerge` only when the merge produced something new, comparing the **decoded `Vehicle` plus its effective per-field versions** - never raw payload bytes, which do not converge across a lossy re-encode and would have swapped one loop for a subtler one. A helper mirrors the merge's own per-field fallback so the comparison and the merge cannot disagree about what "new" means. The `.local` arm's byte comparison is untouched and still serves record-level LWW. Verified by the orchestrator: build 0, swiftlint 0 errors, **1159 tests** (1154 before), and mutation-checked personally - restoring the unconditional `.fieldMerge` fails `rv14PushedVehiclePulledBackUnchangedDoesNotPushAgain` while **both S9 tests keep passing**, which is the right shape: the loop test discriminates and S9 proves genuine merges still push. **`preferences` still loops through the `.local` arm in production** - that half is unfixed and needs its own row |
| **[x]** RV.15 | **DONE 2026-09-03. The `cis` rate feed had never once parsed.** `SourcesFailed=1` on every pass since it was added; the other 23 sources covered the pack, so the only symptom was a Warning that fired every time and that nobody read any more. The source was already the official one - `cbr.ru/scripts/XML_daily.asp` - so this was never about finding a better feed. **CBR serves it as `windows-1251`, and says so in its own XML declaration; .NET Core dropped the legacy codepages from the default encoding set**, so `XDocument` could not resolve the name and threw before reading a single rate. Requirement: default from the system locale at first launch, and a user's Settings choice wins and persists thereafter. **The implementation choice is a real decision and must be made before this is briefed, because the two options differ by an order of magnitude in cost.** **Option A - deep-link to iOS's own per-app language screen.** The app ships `en.lproj` and `ru.lproj`, so iOS already gives it a Language entry in Settings.app, and the OS semantics are *exactly* what was asked: default follows the system, a user override wins, the choice persists. The row becomes a real control that opens `UIApplication.openSettingsURLString`. Cheap, native, no relaunch hack, nothing to keep in sync. **Option B - an in-app picker.** Writing `AppleLanguages` into `UserDefaults` does not take effect until the app restarts, because `Bundle.main` resolves its localisation once at launch - so B either shows a "restart to apply" prompt (poor, and users read it as a bug) or routes **every** string through a per-language `Bundle`. Measured: **268 `Text("literal")` call sites plus 153 `L10n.localize` calls**. `L10n` is already a seam and could take a bundle; SwiftUI's `Text(LocalizedStringKey)` cannot without a custom wrapper on all 268. That is an app-wide refactor touching every screen, and `docs/LOCALIZATION.md` already documents how easily a string slips out of the localised path | Whichever option: L4 asserts the row is **hittable** (it is inert today, so an existence check passes against the bug), that it reaches the language surface, and that a chosen language survives a relaunch. For B, the L1 decision - system locale at first launch, stored preference thereafter - lives in core with its own tests, never inline in the view, and the gate is that a language chosen in-app is what `L10n.localize` returns **without a restart**. `docs/LOCALIZATION.md` and `SCREENMAP.md` (Settings' destinations) move in the same change; screenshots EN and RU  **DECIDED 2026-09-03 (product owner): Option B, the in-app picker, and a restart prompt is acceptable.** So the picker writes `AppleLanguages` into `UserDefaults` and the change lands on the next launch. Four things that decision pulls in. **(a) The prompt is an error-class surface under hard rule 7 and must name its next step** - "Language changes the next time you open Tankbook", not a bare "restart required". **(b) Never call `exit(0)`.** An app that kills itself to apply a setting is an App Store rejection risk and reads as a crash; the user reopens it. **(c) Do NOT half-apply the change to the running session.** Routing `L10n.localize` (153 call sites) to a new bundle while the 268 `Text(LocalizedStringKey)` sites keep the old one would leave the app in two languages at once - visibly worse than one clean restart. All-or-nothing. **(d) Write nothing at first launch.** No stored preference means "follow the system", so a user who later changes their phone's language sees the app follow; storing the system value at first launch would silently freeze it. The product owner's wording was "default from the system setting of the first launch", and this reading satisfies it while keeping the app in step with the phone - flag it if that was not the intent. The screen is therefore telling the truth - `IN RUB ≈ –  converts when online`, `1 entry pending rates`, `SEPTEMBER SPEND 0 ₽` - and will keep telling it forever. **This makes RV.20 ACTIVE rather than latent**: `date_req` support landed today and is verified working for a past date, and this is the backfill that row said was "a question of when, not if" | A backfill that walks the missing dates and fetches each - CBR `?date_req=dd/MM/yyyy` and NBK `?fdate=dd.MM.yyyy`, both verified against 15.08.2026. **Bound it and say what you chose**: the client only ever asks for 400 days, so that is the natural ceiling, but 400 dates x 2 feeds is 800 requests and needs to be paced, idempotent (the insert is already `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) and restartable - a backfill that re-runs from zero on every deploy is its own outage. Decide too whether it runs once or continuously: gaps will recur whenever a feed is down for a day, and carry-forward only covers a gap **after** a first publication exists. L2: with history present for a quote and a gap in the middle, the backfill fills exactly the gap and nothing else; with no history at all, it establishes the window. **Then verify the reported entry actually converts** - a green test with that 2 Sep fill still pending means the chain is not closed  **SHAPE DECIDED 2026-09-03 (product owner): the backfill is DEMAND-DRIVEN, not a fixed window.** The trigger is *"we hold an entry whose `rateDate` has no rate"*, which covers the case that made a fixed window wrong: **import**. `POST /import/parse` lets a user bring in years of MFM history in one action (`docs/API.md`, the rule-9 exception), and those entries can be dated well outside any rolling window - a 400-day ceiling would leave everything older permanently pending, which is the bug this row exists for, reintroduced one day later. So: **the set of dates to fetch is derived from the data, not from the calendar.** Consequences to design for. **(a) The device knows which dates are missing; the server does not** - it holds no entries (hard rule 9), so the *device* must ask, either by requesting a rate for a date it lacks or by widening its pack request. Do not invent a server-side domain query to find them; that is exactly what rule 9 forbids. **(b) An import can request hundreds of distinct dates at once** - bound the burst, dedupe, and make it resumable; a user importing five years must not fire 1800 upstream requests. **(c) The upstream has no rate for a weekend or a holiday**, and asking again every launch would be a permanent retry loop - a date answered "nothing published" must be recorded as answered, distinct from "not yet asked". **(d) Backfilled rows are `published` when they come from a real document for that date and `:carried-forward` only when they do not** - the distinction RV.20 established.  **GAP RULE DECIDED 2026-09-03 (product owner): a date with no published rate takes the most recent earlier known rate, within a bounded window.** This is the right shape and it dissolves the "answered-empty vs not-yet-asked" bookkeeping entirely - a weekend resolves from Friday and is finished, so nothing retries. Look **backwards only**: the rate in force on the day of the fill is one that already existed; a later rate did not. The row is stored `:carried-forward`, never `published` (RV.20). **The window was proposed as 7 days and MEASURED AGAINST THE LIVE FEED as too short.** CBR does not publish across the Russian New Year: asking `date_req` for 01, 05, 08, 09, 10 and 12 January 2026 all return the document dated **31.12.2025**, and the first January document is **13.01.2026** - a **13-day gap**. A 7-day window would leave 07-12 January permanently unresolved, in **RUB**, which is a home currency for this app. **Use 14 days** (13 measured, one day of margin) and pin the January window as a test fixture - the holiday document is already captured at `backend/tests/.../Fixtures/cbr-xml-daily-2026-01-04-holiday-windows1251.xml`. **The bound is not decoration**: without it an imported entry predating all rate history would silently take some unrelated rate rather than staying honestly pending. **And carrying back must not hide a broken feed** - RV.15 went unnoticed for weeks because nothing downstream looked wrong; `SourcesFailed` must still be reported even while gaps are being filled. |

It does not distinguish a **published** row from a `:carried-forward` one, so once a placeholder occupies a slot, the genuine rate published later that same day **can never take its place**. **This is systematic, not occasional**: ECB publishes ~16:00 CET, the job runs every 6 h from process start, and any deploy or restart in the morning makes the day's first pass carry forward. The 2026-09-03 data is the proof - **29 ECB quotes for that date are all `ecb:carried-forward`**, i.e. 09-02's rates standing in for 09-03, and no later pass can correct them. Hard rule 3 makes `rateDate` the entry date, so a fill on the 3rd is converted at the 2nd's rate, silently and permanently | A **published** row must be able to supersede a `:carried-forward` row for the same slot, while a published row must still never overwrite another published row - the append-only guarantee is about real data, not placeholders. `RateSources.IsCarried` already exists to make the distinction. **Entry snapshots are NOT rewritten** (hard rule 3: snapshots immutable, backfill fill-blanks-only) - an entry already converted at the carried rate keeps it, and that is correct; this only stops future conversions inheriting a stale number. L2: a carried row is replaced by a later published row for the same date/base/quote; a published row is **not** replaced by another published row; a carried row is not replaced by another carried row. Mutation-checked - restoring the blanket `DO NOTHING` must fail the first  **DONE 2026-09-03.** `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE ... WHERE exchange_rates.source LIKE @CarriedLike AND NOT @IsCarried` - a published row supersedes a carried placeholder, and only that. The append-only guarantee for real data is untouched: published never overwrites published, carried never overwrites carried. `RateSources` gained the suffix as the single source of truth rather than a literal at the call site. Verified by the orchestrator: `dotnet build` 0, `dotnet format` 0, **339 tests 0 skipped** (336 before), and mutation-checked personally - restoring the blanket `DO NOTHING` fails **1 of 3** tests, the supersede case, while **both negatives still pass**, which is the discrimination that proves they guard the append-only rule rather than the new behaviour. No stored `money` value is rewritten (hard rule 3: snapshots immutable) - an entry already converted at the carried rate keeps it |

On the second device the vehicle tombstone applies cleanly - and then S5's `resurrectReferencedVehicles`, which is gated only on `entityType != Vehicle`, fires for **each co-tombstoned entry and reminder** and brings the car back as ARCHIVED, marked dirty. B pushes the resurrected car back with a newer `updatedAt`, the server tombstone is overwritten, and A pulls its own deleted car back. **The deletion sticks on neither device**, and both land in S5's "came back from another device - it stays archived" state although nobody logged anything after the delete. The empty garage survives a pull only for a car with no history | **Skip resurrection for a pulled record that is itself tombstoned** (`remote.deleted == true`): the cascade must not resurrect its own victim. S5 exists for the case where the other device logged a **new, live** entry to a car this device deleted - that case must keep working exactly as it does, banner and all. **Do not** solve it by ordering the stream or by making the client re-delete: an echo that self-corrects is still an echo, and a device that re-pushes a delete every cycle is [RV.97]'s shape again | L1: applying a pulled tombstoned entry that references a locally-tombstoned vehicle leaves the vehicle tombstoned and NOT dirty. L1 (the case that must not regress): a pulled **live** entry referencing a locally-tombstoned vehicle still resurrects it as archived and still surfaces the S5 banner. L2: two devices, delete-last-car on A, pull on B - the car stays gone on both, and neither pushes it back. **Vacuous traps**: testing only the tombstoned-entry case, which would let a fix that breaks S5 pass; asserting the vehicle's `deletedAt` without asserting it is not dirty (a dirty tombstone still echoes); a fixture whose car has no entries, where the cascade this row is about does not exist |
| **[x]** RV.105 | **`POST /sync/push` costs 578 ms PER RECORD server-side, and it is four seconds from re-entering [RV.97]'s livelock.** Production log, 2026-09-07 05:08-05:17 (device `787c4f6f`, build `1.0.0+806`, the owner's 513-row MFM import): three pushes, **69 records / 39 841 ms**, **93 records / 53 688 ms**, **92 records / 53 140 ms** - all `Accepted=…, Conflicts=0, Rejected=0`. That is **577, 577 and 578 ms per record**, linear to three significant figures, so it is a **fixed per-record cost**, not payload size and not CPU. Pulls in the same minutes return 69 records in **1 021 ms**, so push is ~50x more expensive per record than pull. **The cause is one transaction per record**: `SyncRepository.ApplyChangeAsync` opens a transaction, does `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, allocates an SCN, writes, and commits - **once for every change in the batch** (`SyncRepository.cs:125-137`), while `SyncService` loops the changes one at a time (`SyncService.cs:70,100`). A 92-record batch is therefore 92 serialized commits, and ~578 ms is the fsync-and-round-trip cost of one commit on this host. **The four-second margin, calculated not guessed**: [RV.97] gave the client a 120 s upload budget and capped a batch at 64 KB **or 200 records, whichever binds first**. These fills are large enough that 64 KB binds at ~90 records - but a batch of smaller records (a delete cascade, reminders, preferences) binds on the COUNT, and **200 x 578 ms = 115.6 s against a 120 s budget**. One slower disk day, or one more index, and the client abandons a push the server is still committing - which is exactly [RV.97], at a bigger number. **[RV.97] is confirmed working in the same log** (bounded batches, every one accepted, no `499`); this row is the cost it exposed | **Amortize the commits without losing per-record outcomes - that tension IS the row.** One transaction for the whole batch is the obvious fix and it breaks a stated contract: `docs/SYNC.md:668` promises **partial batch acceptance** (idempotent by id + `baseScn`), so a single conflicting record must not roll back the other 91. **And the per-record transaction is load-bearing for a second reason the code states**: the class comment says SCNs are allocated inside the row's own transaction so that "committed SCNs are contiguous and commit in order, which is what lets a pull cursor page the stream without ever skipping an in-flight commit" - a redesign must keep that or the pull cursor can skip a record forever. Shapes worth measuring, in order: **one transaction per batch with per-record outcomes collected inside it** (conflicts become no-ops within the transaction rather than rollbacks); **a batched SCN allocation** (one range for the batch instead of N allocations); a **multi-row upsert** for the accepted set once conflicts are known. **Measure, do not assume**: report ms per record before and after on a batch of 92, and say which change bought what. **Do not** solve it by lowering [RV.97]'s record cap - that hides the cost behind more round trips and leaves the per-record price untouched | L2 (the row's point): a push of **200 records** - the maximum a client may legally send - completes **well inside** the 120 s client budget, with the measured ms/record reported before and after. L1/L2: a batch containing one conflicting record still returns `accepted` for the others and `conflict` for that one (partial acceptance, `SYNC.md:668`). L2: SCNs assigned by a batch remain contiguous and in commit order, and a pull cursor paging the stream during concurrent pushes never skips a record. **Vacuous traps**: timing a batch of 5 records, where 578 ms x 5 is invisible; asserting the total duration fell without reporting **ms per record**, which is the number that predicts the 200-record case; making the batch atomic and calling partial acceptance an acceptable loss without a product-owner decision written here; testing on a laptop SSD and concluding the fsync cost is gone - the production host is the measurement that matters |

Decimal.zero` (`LogStream.swift:319-331`), so a **rate-pending** row - amount known, home-currency value not yet resolved - is added as **zero**, and the divider renders that sum as a fact. A month with four unconverted fills says the user spent nothing. The `PendingRatesFootnote` does exist and counts the pending rows (`PendingRatesFootnote.swift:22`), but it is a **bare `Text`** - it names no next step and offers no action (hard rule 7) | **Stop reporting a total the data cannot support, and give the pending state a next step.** A month containing rate-pending rows must not print a bare figure: show the partial with its pending count, or omit the number and say why - decide which and write it down, but **`0 €` is not an option**. The account-wide backfill DOES re-run at every launch (`AppRates.refresh` -> `runBackfill`, via the root's automatic pass), but it can only fill from rates the store actually holds. And the owner's server log shows the rate archive being populated **50 dates per run** (`rates.backfill Processed=50 Published=100`, 05:15 and 05:20), so at import time (05:10-05:17) the server may simply not have had June and July yet. **So determine, on the owner's real data: do these rows resolve on a later launch once the server has published those dates?** If yes, the fix is the honest total plus a truthful "waiting for rates" surface. If no, there is a second defect and it needs finding - a one-shot drain that misses is permanent, because nothing scoped to the imported rows ever runs again | L1 (the certain half): a month whose rows are rate-pending does **not** report `0 €` - assert the rendered divider for a month of pending rows, and for a MIXED month (some converted, some pending) assert the figure is marked partial rather than silently short. L1: a fully-converted month is unchanged, to the cent. L4: the pending footnote offers its next step and the action does something observable. L2/L4 for the open question once its answer is established. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the total is not zero without asserting what it IS - a wrong non-zero number is no better; testing a month where every row converted, which passes today; filling with today's rate to make the assertion pass, which reintroduces [RV.88]; asserting the footnote's COUNT rather than that it now names a next step |

L1: a fixture where the printed total is present but WRONG on the paper (a real thing on mixed receipts, hard rule 4) does not regress. **Vacuous traps**: fixing it by removing receipt-052, which deletes the evidence; widening the tolerance until 649.92 counts as 2249.92; asserting the total is 2249.92 on this one file rather than that the precedence rule holds; assuming Vision's confidence means anything - it was 1.00 on the misread |
